=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Note: This problem has almost the same setting as Problem F. Only the parts in bold in the main text and constraints differ.
You are holding a ring with both hands.
This ring consists of N\ (N \geq 3) parts numbered 1,2,\dots,N, where parts i and i+1 (1 \leq i \leq N-1) are adjacent, and parts 1 and N are also adjacent.
Initially, your left hand is holding part 1, and your right hand is holding part 2.
In one operation, you can do the following:

- Move one of your hands to an adjacent part of the part it is currently holding. However, you can do this only if the other hand is not on the destination part.

The following figure shows the initial state and examples of operations that can and cannot be made from there. The number written on each part of the ring represents the part number, and the circles labeled L and R represent your left and right hands, respectively.

You need to follow Q instructions given to you in order.
The i-th (1 \leq i \leq Q) instruction is represented by a character H_i and an integer T_i, meaning the following:

- Perform some number of operations (possibly zero) so that your left hand (if H_i is L) or your right hand (if H_i is R) is holding part T_i.
  Here, you must not move the other hand not specified by H_i.

It is guaranteed that only achievable instructions are given.

Details
Under the settings of this problem, it can be proved that the positions of both hands are uniquely determined just before following the i-th instruction for each i.
At that time, if we denote the positions of the left and right hands as parts l_i and r_i, respectively, it is guaranteed that T_i \neq r_i when H_i is L, and T_i \neq l_i when H_i is R.


Find the minimum total number of operations required to follow all the instructions.

Input

The Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N Q
H_1 T_1
H_2 T_2
\vdots
H_Q T_Q

Output

Print the minimum total number of operations required to follow all the instructions.

Constraints


- 3 \leq N \leq 100
- 1 \leq Q \leq 100
- H_i is L or R.
- 1 \leq T_i \leq N
- N, Q, and T_i are integers.
- Only achievable instructions are given (see the problem statement for details).

Sample Input 1

6 3
R 4
L 5
R 6

Sample Output 1

8


By performing the following operations, you can follow all Q instructions in order.

- Move your right hand as part 2 \rightarrow 3 \rightarrow 4 to follow the first instruction.
- Move your left hand as part 1 \rightarrow 6 \rightarrow 5 to follow the second instruction.
- Move your right hand as part 4 \rightarrow 3 \rightarrow 2 \rightarrow 1 \rightarrow 6 to follow the third instruction.

In this case, the total number of operations is 2+2+4=8, which is the minimum.
(Note that when following the third instruction, you cannot move your right hand as part 4 \rightarrow 5 \rightarrow 6.)

Sample Input 2

100 2
L 1
R 2

Sample Output 2

0

There are cases where you can follow the instructions without performing any operations.

Sample Input 3

30 8
R 23
R 26
R 29
L 20
R 29
R 19
L 7
L 16

Sample Output 3

92

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's ensure code uses sys.stdin.read().split() robust. Input format lines. H_i char, T_i int. Use tokens.

Implementation:

import sys

def dist_on_cycle(a,b,block,n):
    d = (b - a) % n
    if d == 0:
        return 0
    # clockwise path length d from a to b; if block lies strictly inside it, use other way
    if 0 < (block - a) % n < d:
        return n - d
    else:
        return d

def main():
    data=sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data: return
    it=iter(data)
    N=int(next(it)); Q=int(next(it))
    l=1; r=2
    ans=0
    for _ in range(Q):
        H=next(it)
        T=int(next(it))
        if H=='L':
            ans += dist_on_cycle(l,T,r,N)
            l=T
        else:
            ans += dist_on_cycle(r,T,l,N)
            r=T
    print(ans)
if __name__ == "__main__": main()

if d == 0: return 0
if (block - a) % n < d: return n-d
return d

Let's maybe prove deterministic state by induction:
Initial state before instruction 1 is (1,2). For instruction i, before state is (l_i,r_i). If H_i=L, after instruction state is (T_i,r_i). If H_i=R, after is (l_i,T_i). Thus before next instruction is determined. The guarantee ensures after state valid. So yes.

Now, if H_i=L and T_i = l_i, then no operations needed. If H_i=L and T_i != l_i, need move left hand from l_i to T_i while right hand at r_i. The left hand can move along the cycle; right hand acts as obstacle. The minimal number of operations is the distance between l_i and T_i in the graph C_N with vertex r_i removed. Since C_N minus one vertex is a path, distance is length of the unique path not containing r_i. We compute.

Let's maybe write code:

import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N, Q = map(int, input().split())
    l, r = 1, 2
    ans = 0
    for _ in range(Q):
        H, T = input().split()
        T = int(T)
        if H == 'L':
            a, b, blocked = l, T, r
            l = T
        else:
            a, b, blocked = r, T, l
            r = T
        d = (b - a) % N
        if d != 0 and (blocked - a) % N < d:
            ans += N - d
        else:
            ans += d
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Wait, in the if H=='L', we update l=T before computing? We set a,b,blocked before update. Good. In code above, if H=='L': a,b,blocked = l,T,r; l=T. Then compute. If H=='R': a,b,blocked = r,T,l; r=T. Good.

Now, let's maybe produce a more robust distance function:

def move_cost(cur, target, other, n):
    d = (target - cur) % n
    if d == 0:
        return 0
    if (other - cur) % n < d:
        return n - d
    return d

Now, let's maybe write final code with function for clarity:

import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0]); Q = int(data[1])
    l = 1
    r = 2
    ans = 0
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(Q):
        H = data[idx]; T = int(data[idx+1]); idx += 2
        if H == 'L':
            cur = l
            other = r
            l = T
        else:
            cur = r
            other = l
            r = T
        d = (T - cur) % N
        if d != 0 and (other - cur) % N < d:
            ans += N - d
        else:
            ans += d
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

This avoids input.readline issues. Good.

Let's write final code carefully:

import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    Q = int(data[1])
    left = 1
    right = 2
    ans = 0
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(Q):
        h = data[idx]
        t = int(data[idx + 1])
        idx += 2

        if h == 'L':
            cur = left
            other = right
            left = t
        else:
            cur = right
            other = left
            right = t

        d = (t - cur) % N
        if d != 0 and (other - cur) % N < d:
            ans += N - d
        else:
            ans += d

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return

    N = int(data[0])
    Q = int(data[1])

    left = 1
    right = 2
    ans = 0
    idx = 2

    for _ in range(Q):
        h = data[idx]
        t = int(data[idx + 1])
        idx += 2

        if h == 'L':
            cur = left
            other = right
            left = t
        else:
            cur = right
            other = left
            right = t

        d = (t - cur) % N

        if d != 0 and (other - cur) % N < d:
            ans += N - d
        else:
            ans += d

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
